∎

6.3.
Orlov’s equivalence induces Chevalley’s isomorphism
S\otimes S\cong\wedge^{*}V

Let
\beta:=\{e_{1},\dots,e_{2n}\}
be a basis of
H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
and let
\{f_{1},\dots,f_{2n}\}
be the dual basis of
H^{1}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})
.
Define
\mu:X\times X\rightarrow X\times X
by
\mu(x,y)=(x+y,y)
.
Then

\displaystyle\mu^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}(e_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{1}^{*}(e_{i})+\pi_{2}^{*}(e_{i}),

\displaystyle\mu^{*}(\pi_{2}^{*}(e_{i}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{2}^{*}(e_{i}).

Let
\nu:H^{*}(X\times X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{*}(\hat{X}\times X,{\mathbb{Z}})
be the isomorphism induced by the equivalence

(\Psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]}\otimes 1)\circ\mu^{*}:D^{b}(X\times X)\rightarrow D^{b}(\hat{X}\times X)
.
Let
\tilde{\varphi}:H^{*}(X\times X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{*}(\hat{X}\times X,{\mathbb{Z}})
be the isomorphism (
2.3.2
).

Lemma 6.3.1
.

The equality
\nu\circ(id\otimes\tau)=\tilde{\varphi}
holds.

Proof.

Given a subset
K:=\{i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{k}\}
of
[1,2n]:=\{1,2,\dots,2n\}
, ordered by the induced ordering of
[1,2n]
so that
i_{t}<i_{t+1}
,
set
e_{K}:=e_{i_{1}}\wedge e_{i_{2}}\wedge\dots\wedge e_{i_{k}}
and
f_{K}:=f_{i_{1}}\wedge f_{i_{2}}\wedge\dots\wedge f_{i_{k}}
.
Let
L=\{j_{1},\dots,j_{\ell}\}
be another subset, with
j_{t}<j_{t+1}
, and define
e_{L}
similarly.
Given a subset
I\subset K
, we denote by
I^{\prime}
its complement in
K
and by
I^{c}
its complement in
[1,2n]
.
Define
\epsilon_{K,L}\in\{-1,0,1\}
by the equality
e_{K}\wedge e_{L}=\epsilon_{K,L}e_{K\cup L}
. So
\epsilon_{K,L}=0
, if
K\cap L\neq\emptyset
. Note that
\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}=(-1)^{\sum(K)-k(k+1)/2}
, where
\sum(K):=\sum_{t=1}^{k}i_{t}
.
Furthermore,
\epsilon_{K^{c},K}=(-1)^{k}\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}=(-1)^{\sum(K)-k(k-1)/2}
.
We have
e_{K}\wedge\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}e_{K^{c}}=(\epsilon_{K,K^{c}})^{2}e_{K\cup K^{c}}=e_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{2n}
. So Poincaré duality
PD_{X}
sends
e_{K}
to
\int_{X}e_{K}\wedge(\bullet)=\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}f_{K^{c}}
.
In this notation we have

\displaystyle\mu^{*}(\pi_{1}^{*}e_{K}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}e_{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\pi_{1}^{*}e_{i_{1}}+\pi_{2}^{*}e_{i_{1}}]\wedge\cdots\wedge[\pi_{1}^{*}e_{i_{k}}+\pi_{2}^{*}e_{i_{k}}]\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}e_{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I,I^{\prime}}\pi_{1}^{*}e_{I}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}(e_{I^{\prime}}\wedge e_{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I,I^{\prime}}\epsilon_{I^{\prime},L}\pi_{1}^{*}e_{I}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}(e_{I^{\prime}\cup L}).

The cohomological action of the functor
\Psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]}:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(\hat{X})
is given by

\Psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]}(e_{K})=\sigma_{K}PD(e_{K})=\sigma_{K}\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}f_{K^{c}},

where
13
13

13

\phi_{\mathcal{P}}:H^{k}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2n-k}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
is equal to
(-1)^{k(k+1)/2+n}PD_{k}
, where
PD_{k}:H^{k}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2n-k}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
is the Poincaré duality isomorphism
[
H2
, Lemma 9.23]
. Furthermore, the composition

H^{k}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{\mathcal{P}}}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{2n-k}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{\mathcal{P}}}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{k}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})

is
(-1)^{k+n}

[
H2
, Cor. 9.24]
. Now,
\Psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}}[n]
is the inverse of
\Phi_{\mathcal{P}}
and so the the cohomological action of
\Psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}}[n]

restricts to
H^{k}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
as
(-1)^{k+n}\phi_{\mathcal{P}}=(-1)^{k(k+3)/2}PD_{k}
.

\sigma_{K}=(-1)^{k(k+3)/2}
.
We get

\nu(\pi_{1}^{*}e_{K}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}e_{L})=\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I,I^{\prime}}\epsilon_{I^{\prime},L}\sigma_{I}\epsilon_{I,I^{c}}\pi_{\hat{X}}^{*}f_{I^{c}}\wedge\pi_{X}^{*}(e_{I^{\prime}\cup L}).

Interchanging
I
and
I^{\prime}
, with respect to which the sum is symmetric, we get

(6.3.1)

\displaystyle\nu(\pi_{1}^{*}e_{K}\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}e_{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I^{\prime},I}\epsilon_{I,L}\sigma_{I^{\prime}}\epsilon_{I^{\prime},(I^{\prime})^{c}}\pi_{\hat{X}}^{*}f_{(I^{\prime})^{c}}\wedge\pi_{X}^{*}(e_{I\cup L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I^{\prime},I}\epsilon_{I,L}(-1)^{\sum(I^{\prime})-|I^{\prime}|}\pi_{\hat{X}}^{*}f_{(I^{\prime})^{c}}\wedge\pi_{X}^{*}(e_{I\cup L}).

Consider next the isomorphism
\tilde{\varphi}:S\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}S\rightarrow\wedge^{*}V
of section

2.3
. We chose
the bilinear pairing
B_{0}(\bullet,\bullet)
on
V
with respect to which
H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
and
H^{1}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})
are isotropic, and satisfing
B_{0}(e_{i},f_{j})=\delta_{i,j}
and
B_{0}(f_{i},e_{j})=0
, for all
1\leq i,j\leq 2n
.
Let
\psi^{\prime}:C(V)\rightarrow{\rm End}(\wedge^{*}V)
send
v
to
L_{v}+\delta_{B_{0}(v,\bullet)}
, where
\delta_{x}
,
x\in V^{*}
, is contraction with
x
.
We denote
\delta_{B_{0}(e_{i},\bullet)}
by
\delta_{e_{i}}
for short using the equality
B_{0}(e_{i},\bullet)=(e_{i},\bullet)_{V}
and the identifiation of
V
with
V^{*}
via
(\bullet,\bullet)_{V}
.
So
\psi^{\prime}(e_{i})=L_{e_{i}}+\delta_{e_{i}}
and
\psi^{\prime}(f_{i})=L_{f_{i}}
. We set
\delta_{K}:=\delta_{e_{i_{1}}}\delta_{e_{i_{2}}}\cdots\delta_{e_{i_{k}}}
.
Let
\psi:C(V)\rightarrow\wedge^{*}V
be
\psi^{\prime}
composed with evaluation at the unit
{\mathbb{1}}
.
Set
\tilde{\varphi}(s\otimes t)=\psi(s[pt_{\hat{X}}]\tau(t))
. Then

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}(e_{K}\otimes e_{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[L_{e_{i_{1}}}+\delta_{e_{i_{1}}}]\circ\cdots\circ[L_{e_{i_{k}}}+\delta_{e_{i_{k}}}]f_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{2n}\wedge\tau(e_{L})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I,I^{\prime}}e_{I}\wedge\delta_{I^{\prime}}(f_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{2n})\wedge(-1)^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}e_{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I,I^{\prime}}(-1)^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}(-1)^{|I|(2n-|I^{\prime}|)}\delta_{I^{\prime}}(f_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{2n})\wedge e_{I}\wedge e_{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I,I^{\prime}}(-1)^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}(-1)^{|I||I^{\prime}|}(-1)^{\sum(I^{\prime})-|I^{\prime}|}\epsilon_{I,L}f_{(I^{\prime})^{c}}\wedge e_{I\cup L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\subset K}\epsilon_{I^{\prime},I}(-1)^{\ell(\ell-1)/2}(-1)^{\sum(I^{\prime})-|I^{\prime}|}\epsilon_{I,L}f_{(I^{\prime})^{c}}\wedge e_{I\cup L},

where in the fourth equality we used the equality
\delta_{I^{\prime}}(f_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{2n})=(-1)^{\sum(I^{\prime})-|I^{\prime}|}f_{(I^{\prime})^{c}}
.
Comparing with (
6.3.1
) we get

(6.3.2)

\nu(\pi_{1}^{*}\left(e_{K})\wedge\pi_{2}^{*}\tau(e_{L})\right)=\tilde{\varphi}(e_{K}\otimes e_{L}).

This completes the proof of Lemma
6.3.1
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma
6.1.1
.

We have

\phi:=(id\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]})\circ\mu^{*}=(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]})\circ\nu=(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]})\circ\tilde{\varphi}\circ(id\otimes\tau),

where the first equality is the definition of
\phi
, the second is the definition of
\nu
, and the third is Lemma
6.3.1
.
∎

Lemma 6.3.2
.

(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}}):H^{d}(\hat{X}\times X)\rightarrow H^{4n-d}(X\times\hat{X})
is equal to
(-1)^{d(d+1)/2}PD_{\hat{X}\times X}
.

Proof.

Keep the notation of the proof of Lemma
6.3.1
.
In particular,
f_{L}=f_{j_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge f_{j_{\ell}}
and

e_{K}=e_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge e_{i_{k}}
are classes of degrees
\ell
and
k
respectively.
We have the equality
\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}=\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}}
. By definition,

(PD_{\hat{X}\times X}(f_{L}\wedge e_{K}),\bullet)=\int_{\hat{X}\times X}(f_{L}\wedge e_{K})\wedge\bullet
.

\displaystyle(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{-1})(f_{L}\wedge e_{K})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell(\ell+1)/2+n}(-1)^{(2n-k)(2n-k+1)/2+n}PD(f_{L})\wedge PD^{-1}(e_{K})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{[k(k+1)+\ell(\ell+1)]/2}(-1)^{k}\epsilon_{L,L^{c}}\epsilon_{K^{c},K}e_{L^{c}}\wedge f_{K^{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{[k(k+1)+\ell(\ell+1)]/2}\epsilon_{L,L^{c}}\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}e_{L^{c}}\wedge f_{K^{c}}

So

\displaystyle((\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{-1})(f_{L}\wedge e_{K}),f_{L^{c}}\wedge e_{K^{c}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{[k(k+1)+\ell(\ell+1)]/2}\epsilon_{L,L^{c}}\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{(k+\ell)(k+\ell+1)/2}(-1)^{kl}\epsilon_{L,L^{c}}\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}.

On the other hand,

\int_{\hat{X}\times X}(f_{L}\wedge e_{K})\wedge(f_{L^{c}}\wedge e_{K^{c}})=(-1)^{kl}\int_{\hat{X}\times X}f_{L}\wedge f_{L^{c}}\wedge e_{K}\wedge e_{K^{c}}=(-1)^{kl}\epsilon_{L,L^{c}}\epsilon_{K,K^{c}}
.
∎

Remark 6.3.3
.

Let
\varsigma:X\times\hat{X}\rightarrow\hat{X}\times X
be the transposition of the factors. The isomorphism
\varsigma_{*}:V=H^{1}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{1}(\hat{X}\times X,{\mathbb{Z}})=V^{*}
is equal to the one induced by the pairing
(\bullet,\bullet)_{V}
. Hence, the composition
(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}})\circ\varsigma_{*}:\wedge^{*}V\rightarrow\wedge^{*}V

is an analogue of the Hodge
*
operator
[
H1
, Sec. 1.2]
.

6.4.
Hodge-Weil classes on
X\times\hat{X}
from tensor squares of even pure spinors

Let
P\subset S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}
be a rational plane satisfying Assumption
2.4.1
. We keep the notation of Section
2.2
. In particular
\ell_{i}\in{\mathbb{P}}(P)
,
i=1,2
, are the two complex conjugate pure spinors,
\tilde{\ell}_{i}\subset P_{K}
is the
1
-dimensional subspace corresponding to
\ell_{i}
, and
W_{i}\subset V_{K}
is the corresponding maximal isotropic subspace.
The subspace
P\otimes P
of
S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is a trivial
{\rm Spin}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})_{P}
sub-representation, but it decomposes as a direct sum of one-dimensional
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
-representations, the two non-trivial distinct complex conjugate characters
\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\otimes 2}
,
\tilde{\ell}_{2}^{\otimes 2}
, and the two trivial characters
\tilde{\ell}_{1}\wedge\tilde{\ell}_{2}
in
\wedge^{2}S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}
and
\tilde{\ell}_{1}\tilde{\ell}_{2}
in
{\rm Sym}^{2}(S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}})
. Note that the
2
-dimensional subspace

HW_{P_{K}}:=\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\oplus\tilde{\ell}_{2}^{\otimes 2}

of
S^{+}_{K}\otimes_{K}S^{+}_{K}
is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Denote by
HW_{P}
the corresponding
2
-dimensional subspace of
S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes S^{+}_{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Let
\rho,\rho^{\prime}:{\rm Spin}(V)\rightarrow GL(\wedge^{*}V)\cong GL(H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}}))
be the two representations given in (
6.1.6
) and (
6.1.7
). Both factor though the image
SO^{+}(V)
of
{\rm Spin}(V)
.
Recall that
\rho
is the graded extension of the natural homomorphism
\rho:{\rm Spin}(V)\rightarrow SO^{+}(V)
.
Given
g\in{\rm Spin}(V),
the automorphism
\rho^{\prime}(g)
preserves the decreasing filtration

F^{k}H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}}):=\oplus_{i\geq k}H^{i}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}}),

0\leq k\leq 4n
. The induced action of
\rho^{\prime}(g)
on the graded summands agrees with that of
\rho(g)
, by Proposition
6.1.2
.

Let
U\subset H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},K)
be an irreducible representation of the restriction of the
\rho^{\prime}
action of
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})
to a subgroup
G\subset{\rm Spin}(V_{K})
.
Let
k(U)
be the maximal integer, such that
U
is contained in
F^{k}H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},K)
. We call
k(U)
the
weight
of
U
. The intersection of
U
with the subspace
F^{k(U)+1}H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},K)
vanishes, as it is a proper subrepresentation of the irreducible representation
U
. Hence,
U
projects injectively
and
G
-equivariantly into an irreducible representation
\hat{U}\subset H^{k(U)}(X\times\hat{X},K)
of
G
via the
\rho
action of
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})
. If
U
is reducible, but each irreducible
G
-subrepresentation of
U
has the same weight
k_{0}
, we set
k(U)=k_{0}
and let
\hat{U}
be the projection of
U
in
H^{k(U)}(X\times\hat{X},K)
. Again we call
k(U)
the
weight
of
U
. If, furthermore,
U
decomposes as a direct sum of pairwise distinct irreducible subrepresentations of the same weight
k(U)
, then again the projection homomorphism
U\rightarrow\hat{U}
is an isomorphism of
G
representations.
Set
\phi^{\prime}:=\phi\circ(id\otimes\tau):S\otimes S\rightarrow\wedge^{*}V
, where
\phi
given in (
6.1.3
). By definition (
6.1.4
),

\rho^{\prime}_{g}=\phi^{\prime}(m_{g}\otimes m_{g})\phi^{\prime-1}
.

Proposition 6.4.1
.

(1)

The isomorphism
\phi^{\prime}
maps
HW_{P}:=\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\oplus\tilde{\ell}_{2}^{\otimes 2}

into a weight
2n

{\rm Spin}(V)_{P}
-subrepresentation of
H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Q}})
via
\rho^{\prime}
and the projection

\hat{HW}_{P}
of
\phi^{\prime}(HW_{P})
is the rational subspace of
H^{2n}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Q}})
corresponding to the subspace

\wedge^{2n}W_{1}\oplus\wedge^{2n}W_{2}
of
H^{2n}(X\times\hat{X},K)
.

(2)

The weights of
\phi^{\prime}(\ell_{1}\wedge\ell_{2})
and
\phi^{\prime}(\ell_{1}\cdot\ell_{2})
, as
{\rm Spin}(V)_{P}
-subrepresentations via
\rho^{\prime}
, are as follows.
If
n
is even, then the weight of
\phi^{\prime}(\ell_{1}\wedge\ell_{2})
is
2

and the weight of
\phi^{\prime}(\ell_{1}\cdot\ell_{2})
is
0
.
If
n
is odd, then the weight of
\phi^{\prime}(\ell_{1}\cdot\ell_{2})
is
2

and the weight of
\phi^{\prime}(\ell_{1}\wedge\ell_{2})
is
0
.

Proof.

(
1
)
The homomorphism
\phi^{\prime}
is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
. Thus, the two distinct subrepresentations
\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\ell}_{i}^{2})
,
i=1,2
, are complex conjugates and hence have the same weight
k:=k(\tilde{\ell}_{i}^{2})
. The weight is even, as
\phi^{\prime}
is
{\mathbb{Z}}/2{\mathbb{Z}}
graded and
P\otimes P
is contained in
H^{even}(X\times X,{\mathbb{Q}})
.
It follows that
\hat{HW}_{P_{K}}:=\widehat{\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{2})}\oplus\widehat{\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{2})}

is a
2
-dimensional subrepresentation of
H^{k}(X\times\hat{X},K)
, which is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Lemma
2.2.7
shows that the
{\rm Spin}(V)_{P}
-invariant subspace

H^{2i}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Q}})^{{\rm Spin}(V)_{P}}
of
H^{2i}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Q}})

is one-dimensional and it is a trivial
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
character, for
i\neq n
and
0\leq i\leq 4n
.
The space
H^{2n}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{Q}})^{{\rm Spin}(V)_{P}}
is three-dimensional and it is the direct sum of the characters
\det_{1}
,
\det_{2}
and the trivial character of
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
. Hence, the weight of

\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{2})\oplus\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{2})
is
2n
.
We have already seen in the proof of Lemma
2.2.6
that
\tilde{\ell}_{i}^{2}
is the character
\wedge^{2n}W_{i}\cong\det_{i}
of
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
.
It follows that
\widehat{\phi^{\prime}(\tilde{\ell}_{i}^{2})}
is equal to
\wedge^{2n}W_{i}
.

(
2
)

The group
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})
acts transitively on the set of ordered pairs of
complementary maximally isotropic subspaces of
V_{K}
, by
[
Ch
, Sec. 3.3, Lemma 1]
.
The weight is invariant under the
{\rm Spin}(V_{K})
-action.
Hence, it suffices to calculate the weights of the one dimensional representations spanned by
of
\phi^{\prime}(1\wedge[pt_{X}])
and
\phi^{\prime}(1\cdot[pt_{X}])
. We related the isomorphism
\tilde{\varphi}
given in (
2.3.2
) to the isomorphism
\phi

given in (
6.1.3
) in Lemma
6.1.1
.
We have
\phi^{\prime}=(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]})\circ\tilde{\varphi}
. The image
of
([pt_{X}]\otimes 1)-(-1)^{n}(1\otimes[pt_{X}])

via
\tilde{\varphi}
is contained in
F^{4n-2}(\wedge^{*}V)
and not in

F^{4n-3}(\wedge^{*}V)
, by
Lemma
2.3.2
(
1
). Now
(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}\otimes\psi_{{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}[n]})

sends
F^{k}(\wedge^{*}V)
to
F_{4n-k}(\wedge^{*}V)
, by Lemma
6.3.2
, and so the weight is
2
.
Similarly, the weight of the other line is zero, by Lemma
2.3.2
(
2
), via the same argument.
∎

7.
Semiregular twisted sheaves

In Section
7.1
we recall the definition of the semi-regularity map of a coherent sheaf
F
in terms of its Atiyah class
at_{F}
.
A sheaf is semiregular, when its semi-regularity map is injective.
In Section
7.2
we extend the definition of the semi-regularity map for projective bundles. In Section
7.3

we extend the definition for coherent sheaves twisted by a Čech cocycle with coefficients in the local system
\mu_{r}
of
r
-th roots of unity.
We state the analogue of the Buchweitz-Flenner Semiregularity Theorem for twisted sheaves as Conjecture
7.3.9
.
It states that a semiregular twisted sheaf on a special fiber of a family deforms to a twisted sheaf over an open neighborhood of the fiber, provided its Chern character remains of Hodge type.
An analogue of the Semiregularity Theorem for twisted sheaves follows from a very general theorem of Pridham
[
Pr
, Remark 2.6]
. The author’s ignorance prevents him from comparing the two statements, though Conjecture
7.3.9
should follow from Pridham’s result. In Section
7.4
we reduce Conjecture
7.3.9
to the Semiregularity Theorem for untwisted sheaves in the case of families of abelian varieties (and more generally, whenever the Brauer class of the twisted sheaf on the special fiber is the restriction of that of a projective bundle over the family).

7.1.
Semiregular coherent sheaves

Let
E
be a coherent sheaf on a
d
-dimensional complex manifold
M
. Denote the Atiyah class of
E
by
at_{E}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(E,E\otimes\Omega^{1}_{M})
.
The
q
-th component
\sigma_{q}
of the semiregularity map

(7.1.1)

\sigma:=(\sigma_{0},\dots,\sigma_{d-2}):{\rm Ext}^{2}(E,E)\rightarrow\prod_{q=0}^{d-2}H^{q+2}(M,\Omega^{q}_{M})

is the composition

{\rm Ext}^{2}(E,E)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(at_{E})^{q}/q!}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Ext}^{q+2}(E,E\otimes\Omega^{q}_{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Tr}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{q+2}(M,\Omega^{q}_{M}).

The sheaf
E
is said to be
semiregular
, if
\sigma
is injective.
We have the commutative diagram

(7.1.2)

\textstyle{H^{1}(M,TM)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{at_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\rfloor ch(E)}
\textstyle{{\rm Ext}^{2}(E,E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{\prod_{q=0}^{d-2}H^{q+2}(M,\Omega^{q}_{M})}

by
[
BF1
, Cor. 4.3]
.
14
14

14

We follow the convention of
[
T
]
and
[
HL
]
for the sign of the Atiyah class, so that
ch(E)=Tr(at_{E})
, while in
[
BF1
]

ch(E)=Tr(-at_{E})
. So for a vector bundle we choose the Atiyah class to be the extension class of the first jet bundle, rather than the extension class of the bundle of first order differential operators with scalar symbol. See
[
At
, Theorem 5]
.

7.2.
Semiregular projective bundles

Let
B
be a projective
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
-bundle over
M
.
Denote by
{\mathcal{A}}
the Azumaya algebra of
B
. Then
{\mathcal{A}}
is a coherent sheaf of associative algebras with a unit over
M
, locally isomorphic to the sheaf of endomorphisms of a locally free sheaf. If
B={\mathbb{P}}(E)
, for a locally free sheaf
E
over
M
, then

{\mathcal{A}}
is naturally isomorphic to
{\mathcal{E}}nd(E)
. Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
be the kernel of the trace homomorphism
tr:{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{M}
.
We have the direct sum decomposition
{\mathcal{A}}:={\mathcal{A}}_{0}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{M}
, where the second direct summand is generated by the unit.
The sheaf
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
is isomorphic to the adjoint Lie algebra bundle of the principal
PGL(r)
-bundle
Pr(B)
associated to
B
. Denote by
Q
the Atiyah bundle of
Pr(B)

[
At
]
. It fits in the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\rightarrow Q\rightarrow TM\rightarrow 0.

Let
-at_{B}\in H^{1}(M,{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{M})
be the extension class of the above sequence.
If
B={\mathbb{P}}(E)
, then
at_{B}

is the traceless direct summand of
at_{E}
, by
[
At
, page 189 property (1)]
. Consequently,

(7.2.1)

at_{B}=at_{E}-\frac{c_{1}(E)}{r}\cdot id_{E}

(keeping the convention of footnote
14
).

We view the class
at_{B}
as a class in
H^{1}(M,{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{M})
via the inclusion of
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
as a direct summand in
{\mathcal{A}}
. We get the exponential Atiyah class
\exp(at_{B})
with graded summands
at_{k}(B)
in

H^{k}(M,{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\Omega^{k}_{M})
. Note that
at_{0}(B)
is
r
times the unit section and
at_{1}(B)=at_{B}
. Denote by
\kappa(B)
the trace of
\exp(at_{B})
. If
B={\mathbb{P}}(E)
, then

\kappa(B)=ch(E)\exp(-c_{1}(E)/r),

by equation (
7.2.1
).
We get the commutative diagram

(7.2.2)

\textstyle{H^{1}(M,TM)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{at_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\rfloor\kappa(B)}
\textstyle{H^{2}(M,{\mathcal{A}}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{B}}
\textstyle{\prod_{q=1}^{d-2}H^{q+2}(M,\Omega_{M}^{q}),}

where the
semiregularity map

\sigma_{B}
is defined as in equation (
7.1.1
) with
at_{E}
replaced by
at_{B}
. The commutativity of Diagram (
7.2.2
) is proved by the same argument as in
[
BF1
, Cor. 4.3]
establishing the commutativity of Diagram (
7.1.2
).
See Remark
9.3.10
for another proof.

7.3.
Semiregular
\mu_{r}
-twisted sheaves

We refer to
[
Ca1
]
for basic facts about twisted sheaves.
Let
{\mathcal{U}}:=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be an open covering of
M
.
Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a coherent sheaf of non-zero rank
r
over
M
twisted by a Čech cocycle
\tilde{\theta}
in
{\mathcal{Z}}^{2}({\mathcal{U}},\mu_{r})
,
where
\mu_{r}
is the local system of
r
-th roots of unity.
The
\tilde{\theta}
-twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}
has a well defined untwisted determinant line-bundle
\wedge^{r}{\mathcal{B}}
, since
(\tilde{\theta}_{ijk})^{r}=1
.

Example 7.3.1
.

Every projective bundle
B
on
M
admits a lift to such a
\tilde{\theta}
-twisted locally free sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}
with trivial determinant line bundle, where
\tilde{\theta}
is a cocycle representing
the characteristic class
\theta\in H^{2}(M,\mu_{r})
of the bundle. The characteristic class
\theta
is the image of the class of the bundle in
H^{1}(M,PGL(r,{\mathcal{O}}_{M}))
via the connecting homomorphism of the short exact sequence

(7.3.1)

1\rightarrow\mu_{r}\rightarrow SL(r,{\mathcal{O}}_{M})\rightarrow PGL(r,{\mathcal{O}}_{M})\rightarrow 1.

Note that the group of line bundles of order
r
in
{\rm Pic}^{0}(M)
acts transitively on the set of isomorphism classes of choices of lifts
{\mathcal{B}}
.
Note that if
{\mathcal{B}}
happens to be untwisted, so that
B
admits a lift to a vector bundle
{\mathcal{B}}
with trivial determinant, then the Atiyah class of the principal
SL(r)
-bundle associated to
{\mathcal{B}}
and the Atiyah class of the projective bundle
B
are equal, by

[
At
, page 189 property (1)]
.

Remark 7.3.2
.

Keep the notation of Example
7.3.1
.
If the characteristic class
\theta
of the projective bundle
B

has order
\rho
in
H^{2}(M,\mu_{r})
, then
\rho
divides
r
. Set
k=r/\rho
. The short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mu_{\rho}\rightarrow\mu_{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\bullet)^{\rho}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mu_{k}\rightarrow 0
yields exactness of

H^{2}(M,\mu_{\rho})\rightarrow H^{2}(M,\mu_{r})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\bullet)^{\rho}}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{2}(M,\mu_{k}).

Hence,
\theta
is the image of a class
\theta^{\prime}\in H^{2}(M,\mu_{\rho}).

Let
\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}
be a Čech
2
-cocycle with coefficients in
\mu_{\rho}
, which is cohomologous in
Z^{2}({\mathcal{U}},\mu_{r})
to the cocycle
\tilde{\theta}
in Example

7.3.1
,
\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}=\tilde{\theta}\delta(\alpha)
, for a Čech
1
-co-chain
\alpha
with coefficients in
\mu_{r}
. Multiplying the gluing transformations of
{\mathcal{B}}
by the co-chain
\alpha
we get the
\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}
-twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
, still with trivial determinant, with

{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})\cong{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}})\cong B
.
We can thus choose the Čech
2
-cocycle
\tilde{\theta}
in Example
7.3.1

to have coefficients in
\mu_{\rho}
.

Construction 7.3.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a coherent sheaf of rank
r>0
over
M
. Let
{\mathcal{U}}:=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be an open covering, in the analytic topology, with each
U_{i}
biholomorphic to a polydisc and with simply connected finite intersections. Denote by
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
the restriction of
{\mathcal{E}}
to
U_{i}
and let

\psi_{i}:\wedge^{r}{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\rightarrow}}{\mathcal{O}}_{U_{i}}
be a trivialization of the determinant line bundle.
The line bundle
\wedge^{r}{\mathcal{E}}
is represented by the Čech cocycle
\eta_{ij}:=(\psi_{i})_{\mid_{U_{ij}}}\circ(\psi_{j})_{\mid_{U_{ij}}}^{-1}
.
Choose an
r
-th root
\tilde{\eta}_{ij}
of the invertible holomorphic function

\eta_{ij}
.
Let
\varphi_{ij}:({\mathcal{E}}_{j})_{\mid_{U_{ij}}}={\mathcal{E}}_{\mid_{U_{ij}}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{\mid_{U_{ij}}}=({\mathcal{E}}_{i})_{\mid_{U_{ij}}}
be multiplication by
\tilde{\eta}_{ij}^{-1}
.
Then
\theta_{ijk}:=(\tilde{\eta}_{ij}\tilde{\eta}_{jk}\tilde{\eta}_{ki})^{-1}
is a Čech cocycle

\theta
in
{\mathcal{Z}}^{2}({\mathcal{U}},\mu_{r})
and

{\mathcal{B}}:=(\{{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\},\{\varphi_{ij}\})
is a
\theta
-twisted sheaf.
The line bundle
\wedge^{r}{\mathcal{B}}
is trivial, as its transition functions
are

\tilde{\eta}_{ij}^{-r}\psi_{i}\circ\psi_{j}^{-1}=(\psi_{j}\circ\psi_{i}^{-1})\circ\psi_{i}\circ\psi_{j}^{-1}=1.

Note that the
2
-cocycle
\theta
represents the image of the class of
\det({\mathcal{E}})^{-1}
under the connecting homomorphism
H^{1}(M,{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\times})\rightarrow H^{2}(M,\mu_{r})
associated to the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mu_{r}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\bullet)^{r}}}{{\rightarrow}}{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\times}\rightarrow 0.

If the class
[\theta]\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})
has order
\rho
, then
\rho
divides
r
and we may assume that the cocycle
\theta
has coefficients in the local system

\mu_{\rho}\subset\mu_{r}
, by the construction in Remark
7.3.2
.
The above construction goes through more generally for a twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{E}}=(\{{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\},\phi_{ij})
, by a cocycle
\theta^{\prime}\in Z^{2}({\mathcal{U}},\mu_{r})
, in which case the resulting sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}:=(\{{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\},\phi_{ij}\tilde{\eta}_{ij}^{-1})
with a trivial determinant line bundle is
\theta^{\prime}\theta
-twisted.

Remark 7.3.4
.

Note that for the twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}
in Construction
7.3.3

we have the natural isomorphism
{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\Omega_{M})\cong{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\Omega_{M})
,
since
R{\mathcal{H}}om({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{B}})
and
R{\mathcal{H}}om({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{E}})
are naturally isomorphic.

Let
M
be a smooth variety,
{\mathcal{U}}:=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
an open covering, and
\theta:=(\theta_{ijk})_{i,j,k\in I}
a Čech
2
-cocycle with coefficients in the local system
\mu_{r}
. Let
\Delta_{M}\subset M\times M
be the diagonal and let
{\mathcal{M}}\subset M\times M
the first order infinitesimal neighborhood of
\Delta_{M}
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{M}}
is the subscheme of
M\times M
with ideal sheaf
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Delta_{M}}^{2}
. Let
\pi_{i}:{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow M
,
i=1,2
, be the two projections and let
\delta:M\rightarrow{\mathcal{M}}
be the inclusion. We have the short exact sequence

(7.3.2)

0\rightarrow\delta_{*}\Omega_{M}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta^{*}}}{{\rightarrow}}\delta_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{M}\rightarrow 0.

Its extension class is called the
universal Atiyah class
and is
a morphism

(7.3.3)

at:\delta_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{M}\rightarrow\delta_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\Omega_{M}}[1]

in
D^{b}(M\times M)
, which we regard as a natural transformation
at:id\rightarrow\Omega_{M}[1]\otimes(\bullet)
of endofunctors of
D^{b}(M)
.
Note that the open coverings

\{\pi_{1}^{-1}(U_{i})\}_{i\in I}
and
\{\pi_{2}^{-1}(U_{i})\}_{i\in I}
of
{\mathcal{M}}
coincide. The
2
-cocycles
(\pi_{1}^{*}\theta_{ijk})_{i,j,k\in I}
and

(\pi_{2}^{*}\theta_{ijk})_{i,j,k\in I}
coincide as well, since the functions
\theta_{ijk}
are locally constant. Hence,
the categories of twisted coherent sheaves
Coh({\mathcal{M}},\pi_{1}^{*}\theta)
and
Coh({\mathcal{M}},\pi_{2}^{*}\theta)
coincide, and so do

D^{b}({\mathcal{M}},\pi_{1}^{*}\theta)
and
D^{b}({\mathcal{M}},\pi_{2}^{*}\theta)
. The morphism
\pi_{2}
is affine. Consequently, the functor

\pi_{2,*}:Coh({\mathcal{M}},\pi_{1}^{*}\theta)\rightarrow Coh(M,\theta)

is well defined as is its derived analogue
R\pi_{2,*}:D^{b}({\mathcal{M}},\pi_{1}^{*}\theta)\rightarrow D^{b}(M,\theta)
. Any object
E
in
D^{b}({\mathcal{M}})
is thus a Fourier-Mukai kernel for an endofunctor
R\pi_{2,*}(L\pi_{1}^{*}(\bullet)\otimes E)
of
D^{b}(M,\theta)
. The universal Atiyah class (
7.3.3
) is thus also a natural transformation of endofunctors of
D^{b}(M,\theta)
.

Definition 7.3.5
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a
\theta
-twisted sheaf on
M
.

The
Atiyah class
15
15

15

The Atiyah class for more general twisted sheaves is defined in
[
Li
, Sec. 6.5.1]
.

at_{\mathcal{B}}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\Omega_{M}):={\rm Hom}({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\Omega_{M}[1])

of
{\mathcal{B}}
is
the evaluation of the natural transformation (
7.3.3
) on the object
{\mathcal{B}}
.
16
16

16

This agrees with our sign convention in footnote
14
, see
[
At
, Theorem 5]
.

The
first jet sheaf
of
{\mathcal{B}}
is the
\theta
-twisted sheaf

j^{1}({\mathcal{B}}):=\pi_{2,*}(\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{B}})
.
Note that if
{\mathcal{B}}
is locally free, then
at_{\mathcal{B}}
is the extension class
of the short exact sequence of
\theta
-twisted sheaves

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\Omega_{M}\rightarrow j^{1}({\mathcal{B}})\rightarrow{\mathcal{B}}\rightarrow 0

associated to the short exact sequence (
7.3.2
) of Fourier-Mukai kernels in
D^{b}({\mathcal{M}})
.
The Atiyah class of a complex of locally free sheaves is defined in
[
HL
, Sec 10.1]
and the definition goes through for complexes
E^{\bullet}

of
\theta
-twisted locally free sheaves, hence for objects in
D^{b}(M,\theta)
. One has the identity

(7.3.4)

at_{E\otimes F}=at_{E}\otimes id_{F}+id_{E}\otimes at_{F},

for objects
E,F
in
D^{b}(M,\theta)
(see
[
HL
, Sec 10.1]
).

Definition 7.3.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a
\mu_{\rho}
-twisted sheaf. Define
c_{1}({\mathcal{B}})\in H^{1}(M,{\mathcal{O}}_{M})
as
tr(at_{\mathcal{B}})
and let
ch({\mathcal{B}})\in\oplus_{q}H^{q}(M,\Omega^{q}_{M})
be the trace of the exponential Atiyah class
\exp(at_{\mathcal{B}})
. If
r:={\rm rank}({\mathcal{B}})
is non-zero, set
\kappa({\mathcal{B}}):=ch({\mathcal{B}})\exp(-c_{1}({\mathcal{B}})/r)
.

Note that
c_{1}({\mathcal{B}})
belongs to the image in
H^{1}(M,\Omega^{1}_{M})
of the Neron-Severi group tensored with
{\mathbb{Q}}
. It suffices to show it for
{\mathcal{B}}
of non zero rank
r
. The object
{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes\rho}
is untwisted, and
c_{1}({\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes\rho})=\rho r^{\rho-1}c_{1}({\mathcal{B}})
, by Equation (
7.3.4
). Similarly,

ch({\mathcal{B}})
is a rational class, since

ch({\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes\rho})=ch({\mathcal{B}})^{\rho}
and so
ch({\mathcal{B}})
is the
\rho
-th root with constant term
r
of the Chern character
ch({\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes\rho})
.

Lemma 7.3.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a coherent sheaf of positive rank
r
twisted by a cocycle
\theta
with coefficients in
\mu_{r}
and with a trivial determinant line bundle.
Assume that the image of
\theta
in
H^{2}(M,{\mathcal{O}}_{M}^{\times})
is trivial, so that
{\mathcal{B}}\cong{\mathcal{E}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}
, where
{\mathcal{E}}
is an untwisted rank
r
coherent sheaf and
{\mathcal{L}}
is a rank
1
locally free
\theta
twisted sheaf. Then the following equality holds

(7.3.5)

at_{\mathcal{B}}=at_{\mathcal{E}}-\frac{c_{1}({\mathcal{E}})}{r}\otimes id_{\mathcal{E}},

where
\frac{c_{1}({\mathcal{E}})}{r}
is regarded as a class in
H^{1}(M,\Omega^{1}_{M})
.

Proof.

Equation (
7.3.4
) yields the equality
at_{\mathcal{B}}=at_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes id_{\mathcal{L}}+id_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes at_{\mathcal{L}}
and
at_{\mathcal{L}}=c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
, which is
-\frac{c_{1}({\mathcal{E}})}{r}
, since
\det({\mathcal{B}})
is trivial.

∎

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a
\mu_{r}
-twisted sheaf of positive rank
r
of trivial determinant.
In that case
\kappa({\mathcal{B}})=ch({\mathcal{B}})
.
We will see in
Remark
9.3.10
that
Diagram (
7.2.2
) remains commutative when
at_{B}
is replaced with
at_{\mathcal{B}}
to define
\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}
, and the class
\kappa(B)
is replaced with the characteristic class
\kappa({\mathcal{B}})
.

Definition 7.3.8
.

A projective bundle
B
(or a twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}
, twisted by a Čech
2
-cocycle with coefficients in
\mu_{r}
)
is said to be
semiregular
, if the semiregularity map
\sigma_{B}
(resp.
\sigma_{\mathcal{B}}
) is injective.

The following is the analogue of
[
BF1
, Th. 5.1]
for projective bundles and for twisted sheaves. It should follow from
[
Pr
, Remark 2.26]
, though one needs to reconcile the different terminology. In
[
Pr
]
the semiregularity map takes values in Deligne cohomology. See also
[
HP
, Theorem 14.4]
for an application of Pridham’s semiregularity theorem for perfect twisted objects.

Conjecture 7.3.9
.

Let
\pi:{\mathcal{M}}\rightarrow S
be a deformation of a smooth complex projective variety
M_{0}
over a smooth germ
(S,0)
and set
M_{s}:=\pi^{-1}(s)
for
s\in S
. Assume that
{\mathcal{B}}
is a semiregular rank
r
coherent sheaf over
M_{0}
, twisted by a cocycle with coefficients in
\mu_{r}
,
such that for all
p
the class
ch_{p}({\mathcal{B}})

extends to a horizontal section of
R^{2p}\pi_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}
, which belongs to the direct summand
R^{p}\pi_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\pi}
under the Hodge decomposition. Then
{\mathcal{B}}
extends
to a twisted coherent sheaf over
\pi^{-1}(U)
for some open analytic neighborhood

U
of
0
in
S
.

Note that
ch({\mathcal{B}})
remains of Hodge type, if and only if both
c_{1}({\mathcal{B}})
and
\kappa({\mathcal{B}})
remain of Hodge type. The locus where
\kappa({\mathcal{B}})
remains of Hodge type contains the one where
ch({\mathcal{B}})
does. If
\kappa({\mathcal{B}})
remains of Hodge type over
S
, but
ch({\mathcal{B}})
does not,
we can use Construction
7.3.3
to replace
{\mathcal{B}}
with a twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
with trivial determinant, which is the tensor product of
{\mathcal{B}}
with a twisted line bundle. The sheaf
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
satisfies
\kappa({\mathcal{B}})=\kappa({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
satisfies the hypotheses of Conjecture
7.3.9
, since
\kappa({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})=ch({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime})
.

7.4.
The Semiregularity Theorem for
\mu_{r}
-twisted sheaves on abelian varieties

In this section we prove Conjecture
7.3.9
in case
\pi
is a family of abelian varieties.
The proof establishes Conjecture
7.3.9
, more generally, whenever the Brauer class
[\theta]\in H^{2}(M_{0},\mu_{r})
of
{\mathcal{B}}
is the restriction of the Brauer class of a projective bundle over
\pi^{-1}(U)
, for some open neighborhood
U
of
0
in
S
.
Note
17
17

17

I thank Nick Addington for this comment.

that if
{\mathcal{M}}
is quasi projective and
[\theta]
is the restriction of a Brauer class on
{\mathcal{M}}\times_{S}\tilde{S}
, for some finite étale cover
\tilde{S}\rightarrow S
, then such a projective bundle exists, by Gabber’s theorem
[
dJ
]
.

7.4.1.
Construction of a projective bundle

Let
X
be an abelian variety.
The composition

(7.4.1)

H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{2}(X,\frac{1}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}/{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}})

is injective, where the left arrow is the isomorphism induced by the inverse of the sheaf isomorphism

\exp(2\pi i(\bullet)):\frac{1}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mu_{r}
and the right arrow is induced by the inclusion
\frac{1}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}\subset{\mathbb{Q}}
.
The injectivity of the right arrow follows from the snake lemma applied to

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,\frac{1}{r}{\mathbb{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,\frac{1}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}/{\mathbb{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where the right exactness of the horizontal rows follows from the Universal Coefficient Theorem and the torsion freeness of
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
, which implies also the injectivity of the middle vertical arrow.
Given a
{\mathbb{P}}^{r-1}
bundle
B
over
X
we denote by
[B]\in H^{1}(X,PGL(r,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}))
its isomorphism class and by

\bar{c}_{1}(B)\in H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})
the image of
[B]
via the connecting homomorphism of the short exact sequence (
7.3.1
).
Denote by

\delta(B)

the image of
\bar{c}_{1}(B)
in
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}})
via (
7.4.1
).

Lemma 7.4.1
.

Let
\pi:{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow S
be a smooth and proper morphism of smooth analytic spaces all of which fibers are connected abelian varieties. Let
X
be the fiber of
\pi
over
0\in S
.
Given a class
\delta_{0}\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}})
, there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
0
in
S
and a holomorphic projective bundle
p:{\mathbb{P}}\rightarrow\pi^{-1}(U)
,
such that
\delta({\mathbb{P}}_{\mid_{X}})=\delta_{0}
.

Proof.

Step 1:

Assume first that
\delta_{0}
is the image of a class
\frac{1}{r}\tilde{\delta}_{0}
via the natural homomorphism

H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}/{\mathbb{Z}})
,
where
\tilde{\delta}_{0}\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
is a primitive class of Hodge type
(1,1)
. Then there exists a simple semi-homogeneous vector bundle
Q
over
X
with
\delta({\mathbb{P}}(Q))=\delta_{0}
,
by
[
Mu4
, Theorem 7.11(1)]
. The rank
r(Q)
of
Q
is divisible by
r
and divides
r^{n}
, where
n
is the dimension of
X
.
Let
\Sigma(Q)\subset\hat{X}
be the subgroup
\{L\ :\ Q\otimes L\cong Q\}
.
Choose an origin for
X
and denote by
X_{r}
the subgroup of
X
of
r
-torsion points. We have the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow X_{r}\cap K(\tilde{\delta}_{0})\rightarrow X_{r}\rightarrow\Sigma(Q)\rightarrow 0,

where
K(\tilde{\delta}_{0})
is the kernel of the homomorphism
\phi_{D}:X\rightarrow\hat{X}
associated to a line-bundle
D
with
c_{1}(D)=\tilde{\delta_{0}}
,
by
[
Mu4
, Theorem 7.11(4)]
.
Furthermore,
{\mathcal{E}}nd(Q)\cong\oplus_{L\in\Sigma(Q)}L
, by
[
Mu4
, Proposition 7.1]
.

The subgroup
\Sigma(Q)
consists of isomorphism classes of line bundles on
X
. However,
we may and do regard
\Sigma(Q)
as subgroup of autoequivalences of
D^{b}(X)
endowed with a linearization, i.e., a choice of a line-bundle representing each isomorphism class in
\Sigma(Q)
together with isomorphisms between
the composite functor
L_{1}\otimes(L_{2}\otimes(\bullet))
and
L_{3}\otimes(\bullet)
, for all
L_{1},L_{2},L_{3}\in\Sigma(Q)
such that
L_{3}
is isomorphic to
L_{1}\otimes L_{2}
, and these isomorphisms of functors satisfy the natural associativity axioms. Such a linearization is provided by conjugating the action on
D^{b}(\hat{X})
of the group
\Sigma(Q)
of translation automorphisms of
\hat{X}
via the equivalence
\Phi_{\mathcal{P}}:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(\hat{X})
with the Poincare line bundle
{\mathcal{P}}
as a Fourier-Mukai kernel. Alternatively, it is provided by the Appell-Humbert theorem
[
BL
, Theorem 2.2.3]
.

Set
{\mathcal{A}}:=\oplus_{L\in\Sigma(Q)}L
.
Let
a:{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{A}}
be a homomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules. Denote by

\lambda,\rho:{\mathcal{A}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}nd({\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}})

the homomorphism
\lambda(s)=a(s,(\bullet))
and
\rho(s)=a((\bullet)\otimes s)
. Then
a
is the multiplication for the structure of an Azumaya algebra on
{\mathcal{A}}
, if and only if
a
endows
{\mathcal{A}}
with the structure of a sheaf of associative algebras with a unit, and

\lambda\otimes\rho:{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{\circ}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}nd({\mathcal{A}})
is an isomorphism of sheaves of algebras (see
[
Mi1
, Proposition IV.2.1]
).
Here
{\mathcal{A}}^{\circ}
is
{\mathcal{A}}
endowed with the multiplication
a^{\circ}
obtained by composing
a
with the transposition of the tensor factors of

The condition that

-modules satisfying

Given

Thus, the conditions that

8.
Secant sheaves on abelian threefolds

In Section
8.1
we enumerate the
K
-secants spanned by rational Hodge classes for a generic principally polarized abelian variety.
In Section
8.2
we consider examples of ideal sheaves twisted by a line-bundle, which are secant sheaves over abelian threefolds and fourfolds.
In Section
8.3
we consider the ideal sheaf
F:={\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{d+1}C_{i}}
of
d+1
translates
C_{i}
of the Abel-Jacobi
image of a non-hyperelliptic curve
C
of genus
3
in its Jacobian
X={\rm Pic}^{2}(C)
. We show that the obstruction map

ob_{F}:HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)
has rank
6
, and so its kernel is a
9
-dimensional subspace of unobstructed first order deformations of the pair
(X,F)
.

In Section
8.4
we set
F_{1}:={\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{d+1}C_{i}}(\Theta)
and
F_{2}:={\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{d+1}\Sigma_{i}}(\Theta)
,
where
\Sigma_{i}
are images under the natural involution
L\mapsto\omega_{C}\otimes L^{-1}
of
{\rm Pic}^{2}(C)
of translates of the Abel-Jacobi image of
C
.
Let the object
E:=\Phi(F_{1}\boxtimes F_{2})
be the image of the outer tensor product of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
via Orlov’s
equivalence
\Phi:D^{b}(X\times X)\rightarrow D^{b}(X\times\hat{X})
. We show that the kernel of

ob_{E}:HT^{2}(X\times\hat{X})\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(E,E)
is equal to the kernel of the homomorphism
ch(E):HT^{2}(E)\rightarrow H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{C}})

via the
HT^{*}(X\times\hat{X})
-module structure of
H^{*}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{C}})
and the action of
HT^{*}(X\times\hat{X})
on
ch(F)\in HH^{*}(X\times\hat{X},{\mathbb{C}})
.

In Section
8.5
we show that the isomorphism
\Phi^{HT}:HT^{2}(X\times X)\rightarrow HT^{2}(X\times\hat{X})

maps the “diagonally” embedded
\ker(ob_{F_{1}})\subset HT^{2}(X)
to a
9
-dimensional subspace of first order commutative and gerby deformations of
X\times\hat{X}
.

8.1.
K
-secants on a generic ppav

Let
(X,\Theta)
be a principally polazixed abelian
n
-fold with a cyclic Neron-Severi group generated by
\Theta
. Let
\phi_{\Theta}:X\rightarrow\hat{X}
be the isomorphism induced by
\Theta
and set
f:=(\phi_{\Theta}^{*})^{-1}:H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{1}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})
. Given
w\in H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
and
\theta\in H^{1}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})
and identifying
H^{1}(\hat{X},{\mathbb{Z}})
with
H^{1}(Z,{\mathbb{Z}})^{*}
, we have

(8.1.1)

f(w)(f^{-1}(\theta))=-\theta(w).

We have

(8.1.2)

{\rm End}_{\mathbb{Q}}(X\times\hat{X})\cong{\rm End}_{Hdg}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})=\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{11}I_{X}&a_{12}f^{-1}\\
a_{21}f&a_{22}I_{\hat{X}}\end{array}\right)\ :\ a_{ij}\in{\mathbb{Q}}\right\},

where
I_{X}
is the identity endomorphism of
H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
and
I_{\hat{X}}
is defined analogously.
The identity (
8.1.1
) implies that
the group
SO^{+}_{Hdg}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})
is the subgroup of invertible elements of
{\rm End}_{Hdg}(V_{\mathbb{Q}})

with
a_{11}a_{22}-a_{21}a_{12}=1.

Allowing the coefficients to belong to a quadratic imaginary number field
K
and imposing
a_{11}a_{22}-a_{21}a_{12}=1

we get the group
SO^{+}_{Hdg}(V_{K})
.

Lemma 8.1.1
.

The
SO^{+}_{Hdg}(V_{K})
-orbit of the pure spinor
{\rm span}_{K}\{1\}\in{\mathbb{P}}(S^{+}_{K})
is

\{{\rm span}_{K}\{\Theta^{n}\}\}\cup\{{\rm span}_{K}\{\exp(k\Theta)\}\ :\ k\in K\}
.

Proof.

Denote the displayed matrix in (
8.1.2
) by
A
. If
a_{22}=0
, then
A(H^{1}(\hat{X},K))=H^{1}(X,K)
.
Hence, in that case
A
maps the pure spinor
H^{0}(X,K)={\rm span}_{K}\{1\}
of the maximal isotropic subspace
H^{1}(\hat{X},K)
to the pure spinor
H^{2n}(X,K)={\rm span}_{K}\{\Theta^{n}\}

of
H^{1}(X,K)
.
The subgroup of
SO^{+}_{Hdg}(V_{K})
leaving
H^{1}(\hat{X},K)
invariant is the lower triangular subgroup with
a_{12}=0
. Hence, the
SO^{+}_{Hdg}(V_{K})
-orbit
of
{\rm span}_{K}\{1\}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{K}
. It follows that the latter orbit is the
rational normal curve of degree
n
in
{\mathbb{P}}({\rm span}_{K}\{\Theta^{j}\!:\!0\!\leq\!j\!\leq\!n\})
, which is
the Zariski closure
of
\{{\rm span}_{K}\{\exp(k\Theta)\}\!:\!k\in K\}
.
∎

The involution
\iota
interchanging the coefficients of
\frac{\Theta^{j}}{j!}
and
\frac{\Theta^{n-j}}{(n-j)!}
acts on the above set of pure spinors and corresponds to
the equality
k^{n}\exp(k^{-1}\Theta)=\iota(\exp(k\Theta))
. The action of the element
\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-f^{-1}\\
f&0\end{array}\right)
on
{\mathbb{P}}(S_{K})
extends the action of
\iota
.

If
K={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-d})
,
d
a positive integer, and we write
k=(\rho+\tau\sqrt{-d})/q
, with
\rho,\tau,q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
\gcd(\rho,\tau,q)=1
,
q>0
, and
\tau\neq 0
, then the non-rational pure spinors defined over
K
in Lemma
8.1.1
are enumerated by
(\alpha+\tau\sqrt{-d}\beta)/q^{n}
, where

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{n}+\rho q^{n-1}\Theta+q^{n-2}(\rho^{2}-\tau^{2}d)\frac{\Theta^{2}}{2}+q^{n-3}\rho(\rho^{2}-3\tau^{2}d)\frac{\Theta^{3}}{3!}

\displaystyle+q^{n-4}(\rho^{4}-6\rho^{2}\tau^{2}d+\tau^{4}d^{2})\frac{\Theta^{4}}{4!}+\dots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\rho}{q}\Theta\right)\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\frac{n}{2}\rfloor}(-1)^{j}q^{n-2j}(\tau^{2}d)^{j}\frac{\Theta^{2j}}{(2j)!}

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{n-1}\Theta+\rho q^{n-2}\Theta^{2}+q^{n-3}(3\rho^{2}-d\tau^{2})\frac{\Theta^{3}}{3!}+q^{n-4}\rho(\rho^{2}-\tau^{2}d)\frac{\Theta^{4}}{4!}+\dots

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\rho}{q}\Theta\right)\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor\frac{n-1}{2}\rfloor}(-1)^{j}q^{n-1-2j}(\tau^{2}d)^{j}\frac{\Theta^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}

and
\alpha,\beta\in H^{ev}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
. Furthermore,
{\rm span}_{\mathbb{Z}}\{\alpha,\beta\}
is saturated in
H^{ev}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
when
q=1
.

The rational
K
-secant
P:={\rm span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{\alpha,\beta\}
is spanned by Hodge classes, and so a choice of one of the two pure spinors in
{\mathbb{P}}(P)
determines
the structure of an abelian variety of Weil type for
X\times\hat{X}
, by Lemma
2.2.6
. If
a\alpha+b\beta
is the Chern character of an object
F\in D^{b}(X)
, then

\chi(F^{\vee}\otimes F)=\int_{X}(a\alpha+b\beta)^{\vee}(a\alpha+b\beta)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccl}0&\mbox{if}&n\ \mbox{is odd},\\
-2q^{2}(a^{2}\tau^{2}d+b^{2})&\mbox{if}&n=2,\\
8dq^{4}\tau^{2}(a^{2}\tau^{2}d+b^{2})&\mbox{if}&n=4,\\
(-1)^{\frac{n}{2}}2^{n-1}d^{\frac{n}{2}-1}q^{n}\tau^{n-2}(a^{2}\tau^{2}d+b^{2})&\mbox{if}&n\ \mbox{is even.}\end{array}\right.

If
n
is even, the minimum value of
|\chi(F^{\vee}\otimes F)|
above for a given imaginary quadratic number field
K

is
2^{n-1}d^{\frac{n}{2}-1}
, where
d
is the product of distinct positive primes satisfying
K={\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-d})
, and it is obtained for
q=1
,
\tau=\pm 1
,
\rho\in{\mathbb{Z}}
,
a=0
, and
b=1
, provided the class
\beta
is the Chern character of an object
F
. In that case, applying a lift of
\iota
in
{\rm Aut}(D^{b}(X))
, we can get such a representative object with a non-zero rank. When
n=4
and
\dim{\rm Hom}(F,F)=1,

then
\dim{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)=\chi(F^{\vee}\otimes F)+2\dim{\rm Ext}^{1}(F,F)-2
, and so
\chi(F^{\vee}\otimes F)
is relevant for the semi-regularity property of
F
in the absolute or equivariant sense.

8.2.
Examples of secant sheaves

Let
C
be a non-hyperelliptic curve of genus
3
.
Set
X:={\rm Pic}^{2}(C)
and let
\Theta\subset X
be the canonical divisor. The natural morphism
C^{(2)}\rightarrow\Theta
is an isomorphism. Indeed, the morphism is injective,
\Theta
is smooth, by Riemann’s Singularity Theorem
[
BL
, Theorem 11.2.5]
, and so the morphism is an isomorphism by Zariski’s Main Theorem.
Let
AJ:C\rightarrow{\rm Pic}^{1}(X)
be the Abel-Jacobi morphism.
Given a point
t\in{\rm Pic}^{1}(X)
denote by
C_{t}\subset X
the translate of
AJ(C)
by
t
. Denote by
[pt]\in H^{6}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
the class Poincaré-dual to a point.
Given a subvariety
Z
of
X
, denote by
[Z]
the class in
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
Poncaré-dual to
Z
.
Then
[\Theta]^{3}/6=[pt]
and

[C_{t}]=[\Theta]^{2}/2,

by Poncaré’s formula
[
BL
, Sec. 11.2]
. We denote
[\Theta]
by
\Theta
as well.

Let
d
be a positive integer. Set
\alpha:=1-\frac{d}{2}\Theta^{2}
and
\beta:=\Theta-d[pt]
. We have

\exp(\sqrt{-d}\Theta)=1+\sqrt{-d}\Theta-\frac{d}{2}\Theta^{2}-d\sqrt{-d}[pt]=\left(1-\frac{d}{2}\Theta^{2}\right)+\sqrt{-d}\left(\Theta-d[pt]\right)=\alpha+\sqrt{-d}\beta.

Cup product with
\exp(\sqrt{-d}\Theta)
is an automorphism of
S_{K}:=H^{*}(X,K)
, which belongs to the image of
m:{\rm Spin}(V_{K})\rightarrow GL(S_{K})
,
where
m
is given in (
2.1.3
). Now
1\in H^{0}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
is an even pure spinor, hence, so is
\exp(\sqrt{-d}\Theta)
.
Let
t_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq d+1
, be distinct points of
{\rm Pic}^{1}(C)
and set
C_{i}:=C_{t_{i}}
.

Lemma 8.2.1
.

Assume that
C_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq d+1
, are pairwise disjoint. Then the following equality holds

ch\left({\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{d+1}C_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(\Theta)\right)=1+\Theta-\frac{d}{2}\Theta^{2}-d[pt]=\alpha+\beta.

Consequently,
ch\left({\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{d+1}C_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(\Theta)\right)

and
ch\left(R{\mathcal{H}}om({\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{d+1}C_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(\Theta),{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\right)

both belong to the secant line to the spinor variety through the pure spinor
\exp(\sqrt{-d}\Theta)
and its complex conjugate
\exp(-\sqrt{-d}\Theta)
.

Proof.

The equality
ch({\mathcal{O}}_{C_{i}})=\Theta^{2}/2-2[pt]
holds, since
\chi({\mathcal{O}}_{C_{i}})=-2
.
Hence, given
n
disjoint translates
C_{i}
of
AJ(C)
, we get

ch({\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{n}C_{i}})=1-n\left(\Theta^{2}/2-2[pt]\right)=1-\frac{n}{2}\Theta^{2}+2n[pt].

Then

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{I}}_{\cup_{i=1}^{n}C_{i}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(k\Theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\frac{n}{2}\Theta^{2}+2n[pt])(1+k\Theta+\frac{k^{2}}{2}\Theta^{2}+k^{3}[pt])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+k\Theta+\frac{(k^{2}-n)}{2}\Theta^{2}+(k^{3}-3kn+2n)[pt]).

Taking
k=1
and
n=d+1
we get the desired equality.
∎

Example 8.2.2
.

Let
X
be an abelian surface, let
F
be a coherent sheaf with
w:=ch(F)
satisfying
(w,w)_{S}<0
, and let

h\in H^{ev}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
be an algebraic class, such that
(h,w)_{S}=0
and
(h,h)_{S}<0
. Then
{\rm span}_{\mathbb{Q}}\{w,h\}
is a secant to the spinor variety inducing complex multiplication by the imaginary quadratic number field
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-d})
, where
d=\frac{(w,w)(h,h)}{4}
, by
[
M2
, Prop. 1.7]
. In particular,
F
is a
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-d})
-secant sheaf.

Example 8.2.3
.

When
X
is an abelian
n
-fold,
n\geq 4
, Lemma
8.2.1
can be generalized to produce secant sheaves with a secant line inducing complex multiplication by
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-d})
as follows. Let
C
be a Brill-Noether generic curve of genus
n
and set
X:={\rm Pic}^{n-1}(C)
. Denote by
W_{k}
the Abel-Jacobi image of
C^{(k)}
in
{\rm Pic}^{k}(C)
and by
[W_{k}]

the class of any translate of
W_{k}
in
X
. Then
[W_{k}]=\Theta^{n-k}/(n-k)!
, by Poincaré’s formula. Let
t_{jk}
be generic points in
{\rm Pic}^{n-k-1}(C)
.
Let the subscheme
Z
of
X
be the union

(8.2.1)

Z:=\cup_{k=0}^{n-2}\cup_{j=1}^{a_{k}}\tau_{t_{jk}}(W_{k}).

Let
F:={\mathcal{I}}_{Z}(a_{n-1}\Theta)
be the tensor product of the ideal sheaf of
Z
with
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(a_{n-1}\Theta)
.
Let
\alpha
be the real part of
\exp(\sqrt{-d}\Theta)
and
\sqrt{d}\beta
its imaginary part.

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle 1-d[W_{n-2}]+d^{2}[W_{n-4}]+\cdots+(-d)^{n/2}[pt]

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Theta-d[W_{n-3}]+\cdots

The integers
a_{k}
,
0\leq k\leq n-1
, in (
8.2.1
) should be chosen to satisfy the equation

(8.2.2)

ch(F)=\alpha+a_{n-1}\beta.

For example, when
n=4
we may assume that only the irreducible components of
Z
in (
8.2.1
), which are translates of
W_{2}
, intersect and every such pair interests at
\Theta^{4}/4=6
points. Note that
\chi({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{1}})=\chi({\mathcal{O}}_{C})=-3
and

\chi({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{2}})=3
. For the latter equality use that
C
is non-hyperelliptic to conclude that
W_{2}
is isomorphic to
C^{(2)}
, as well as the equalities
h^{0,1}(C^{(2)})=4
and

. We get

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta^{3}/6-3[pt],

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta^{2}/2-\Theta^{3}/3+3[pt]\ \ \mbox{(see Lemma \ref{lemma-ch-O-W-2})},

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{2}a_{k}ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{k}})-6\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}a_{2}\\
2\end{array}\!\!\right)[pt]

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{I}}_{Z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{a_{2}}{2}\Theta^{2}+\left[\frac{2a_{2}-a_{1}}{6}\right]\Theta^{3}+\left[6\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}a_{2}\\
2\end{array}\!\!\right)-3a_{2}+3a_{1}-a_{0}\right][pt],

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{I}}_{Z}(a_{3}\Theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+a_{3}\Theta+\left[\frac{a_{3}^{2}-a_{2}}{2}\right]\Theta^{2}+\left[\frac{a_{3}^{3}-3a_{3}a_{2}+2a_{2}-a_{1}}{6}\right]\Theta^{3}+

\displaystyle\left[a_{3}^{4}-6a_{2}a_{3}^{2}+8a_{2}a_{3}-4a_{1}a_{3}+6\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}a_{2}\\
2\end{array}\!\!\right)-3a_{2}+3a_{1}-a_{0}\right][pt]

\displaystyle\alpha+a_{3}\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+a_{3}\Theta-\frac{d}{2}\Theta^{2}-\frac{da_{3}}{6}\Theta^{3}+d^{2}[pt]

Comparing coefficients in Equation
8.2.2

we get
a_{2}=d+a_{3}^{2}
,
a_{1}=2(d+a_{3}^{2})(1-a_{3})
,

a_{0}=6a_{3}^{4}-6a_{3}^{3}+8da_{3}^{2}-6da_{3}+2d^{2}=(a_{3}^{2}+d)(6a_{3}^{2}-6a_{3}+2d).

We get a secant ideal sheaf tensored with
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(a_{3}\Theta)
for every choice of an integer
a_{3}\leq 1
.
If we choose
a_{3}=1
, then
a_{2}=d+1
,
a_{1}=0
, and
a_{0}=2d(d+1)
.
Once again we can choose
Z
to be invariant with respect to a cyclic subgroup
G
of
X
of order
d+1
.
However,
F_{d}:={\mathcal{I}}_{Z}(\Theta)
is unlikely to be semiregular, and
the semiregularity map could restrict to
{\rm Ext}^{2}(F_{d},F_{d})^{G}
as an injective map for at most finitely many values of
d
,
since
\dim{\rm Ext}^{2}(F_{d},F_{d})=\int_{X}ch(F_{d})ch(F_{d}^{\vee})+2\dim{\rm Ext}^{1}(F_{d},F_{d})-2=8d(d+1)-2+2\dim{\rm Ext}^{1}(F_{d},F_{d})

grows quadratically
with
d
, while the order of
G
is linear in
d
.
Another choice of a
K
-secant object with the same Chern character is the following.
Let
\tau_{t_{0}}(W_{2})
be a translate of
W_{2}
in
X
and set
Z:=\cup_{t\in G}\tau_{t_{0}+t}(W_{2})
.
Let
g
be a generator of
G
and set
t_{i}:=t_{0}+ig
,
0\leq i\leq d
.
We choose
G
so that
for each pair
(i,j)
, with
0\leq i<j\leq d
, the intersection
\tau_{t_{i}}(W_{2})\cap\tau_{t_{j}}(W_{2})
consists of
6
distinct points, and this collection of
\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}d+1\\
2\end{array}\!\!\right)
sets of
6
points are pairwise disjoint. For each such pair
(i,j)

choose
4
of the
6
points of intersection
\tau_{t_{i}}(W_{2})\cap\tau_{t_{j}}(W_{2})
and let
\nu:\tilde{Z}\rightarrow Z\subset X
be the partial normalization of
Z
resolving the self intersection at each of the
4
chosen points for each pair
(i,j)
. Let
F^{\prime}
be the object

(8.2.6)

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu^{*}}}{{\rightarrow}}\nu_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{Z}}.

We choose the collection of
\left(\!\!\begin{array}[]{c}d+1\\
2\end{array}\!\!\right)
sets of
4
points to be
G
-invariant. Note that
ch(F^{\prime}(\Theta))=ch(F_{d}))
.
Let
q:X\rightarrow Y:=X/G
be the quotient map and set
\bar{Z}:=q(\tau_{t_{0}}(W_{2}))
.
It admits the partial normalization
\bar{\nu}:\tilde{Z}/G\rightarrow\bar{Z}
.
Then
Z=q^{-1}(\bar{Z}))
is an etalé
G
-cover of
\bar{Z}
and
F^{\prime}
is the pullback via
q
of the object
\bar{F}^{\prime}:=\left[{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\rightarrow\bar{\nu}_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\tilde{Z}/G)})\right]
.

Example 8.2.4
.

Keep the notation of Example
8.2.3
, let
C
be a non-hyperelliptic curve of genus
4
with two
g^{1}_{3}
’s,
X={\rm Pic}^{3}(C)

let
e:\Theta\subset X
be the theta divisor
W_{3}
, and let
W_{2,p}=\{p+p_{1}+p_{2}\ ;\ p_{1},p_{2}\in C\}
be the translate of
W_{2}\subset{\rm Pic}^{2}(C)
by the point
p
into
X
. Then
W_{2,p}
is contained in
\Theta
and passes through the singularities of
\Theta
(the two
g^{1}_{3}
’s) and is thus a Weil divisor
20
20

20

Let
\iota:X\rightarrow X
be the involution sending
L
to
\omega_{C}\otimes L^{-1}
. Given two distinct points
p,p^{\prime}\in C
, we have the scheme theoretic equality
W_{2,p}\cup\iota(W_{2,p^{\prime}})=\Theta\cap\tau_{p^{\prime}-p}(\Theta)
.
So
W_{2,p}+\iota(W_{2,p^{\prime}})
is a cartier divisor in the linear system of
e^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(\tau_{p^{\prime}-p}(\Theta))
.

in
\Theta
. Let
{\mathcal{I}}_{W_{2,p}}
be the ideal sheaf of
W_{2,p}
as a subvariety of
\Theta
. Choose
d
unordered pairs of points
\{p_{i},q_{i}\}_{i=1}^{d}
in
C
, with
\{p_{i},q_{i}\}\cap\{p_{j},q_{j}\}=\emptyset
, for
i\neq j
. Assume that the line secant to the canonical embedding of
C
through
p_{i}
and
q_{i}
does not lie on the unique quadric containing the canonical curve.
Let
C_{i}=\{p+p_{i}+q_{i}\}\subset\Theta
be
the translate
W_{1}:=AJ(C)\subset{\rm Pic}^{1}(C)
into
\Theta
. Then
C_{i}
is contained in the smooth locus of
\Theta
. Assume that
p\not\in\{p_{i},q_{i}\}
, for all
1\leq i\leq d
. Then
C_{i}\cap W_{2,p}=\{p+p_{i}+q_{j}\}
with multiplicity
21
21

21

Indeed, the scheme theoretic intersection of
C_{i}
with
W_{2,p}\cup\iota(W_{2,p^{\prime}})
is equal to the scheme theoretic intersection of
C_{i}
with
\tau_{p^{\prime}-p}(\Theta)
, which has degree
4
, and
C_{i}\cap\iota(W_{2,p^{\prime}})
is a divisor in the linear system of
\omega_{C}(-p_{i}-q_{i}-p^{\prime})
and so of degree
3
.

1
.
Set
Z_{d}:=W_{2,p}\cup\cup_{i=1}^{d}C_{i}
, considered as a subscheme of
\Theta
. Then
e_{*}{\mathcal{I}}_{Z_{d}}(\Theta)
is a
{\mathbb{Q}}(\sqrt{-d})
-secant sheaf. Indeed, we have:

\displaystyle ch(e_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\Theta}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-ch({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(\Theta))=\Theta-\frac{\Theta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\Theta^{3}}{6}-\frac{\Theta^{4}}{24},

\displaystyle ch(e_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\Theta}(\Theta)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta+\frac{\Theta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\Theta^{3}}{6}+\frac{\Theta^{4}}{24},

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Theta^{2}}{2}-\frac{\Theta^{3}}{3}+\frac{\Theta^{4}}{8}\ \mbox{(see Example \ref{example-secant-sheaf-on-jacobian-of-genus-4-curve})},

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{2}}(\Theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Theta^{2}}{2}+\frac{\Theta^{3}}{6}+\frac{\Theta^{4}}{24},

\displaystyle ch(e_{*}({\mathcal{I}}_{W_{2,p}}(\Theta)))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta,

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Theta^{3}}{6}-\frac{\Theta^{4}}{8}\ \mbox{(see Example \ref{example-secant-sheaf-on-jacobian-of-genus-4-curve})},

\displaystyle ch({\mathcal{O}}_{W_{1}}(\Theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Theta^{3}}{6}+\frac{\Theta^{4}}{24}=[W_{1}]+[pt],

\displaystyle ch(e_{*}{\mathcal{I}}_{Z_{d}}(\Theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta-d\frac{\Theta^{3}}{6}=\beta.

Set
F_{d}:=e_{*}{\mathcal{I}}_{Z_{d}}(\Theta)
.
Note that
\dim{\rm Ext}^{2}(F_{d},F_{d})=-\int_{X}\beta^{2}+2\dim{\rm Ext}^{1}(F_{d},F_{d})-2=8d+2\dim{\rm Ext}^{1}(F_{d},F_{d})-2
grows linearly with
